# PREFACE.

*The author prays for health and a blessing from God for all seekers of the Chemical art, namely the true sons of Hermes <note>original: "Hermetis filijs." In alchemical literature, "Sons of Hermes" refers to the legitimate practitioners and students of the Hermetic tradition, named after the mythical founder Hermes Trismegistus.</note>.*

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial 'C' containing intricate floral and vine patterns.</image-desc>

When I considered within myself, Kind Reader, that many counterfeit books and false "Recipes" of the Alchemists <term>Alchemists: specifically "Alchymistarum" here, often used by the author to distinguish between "puffers" (fraudulent gold-makers) and true philosophers of nature</term>—as they are called—composed through the fraud and greed of impostors, circulate among the seekers of natural and hidden arts, in which not even a spark of truth shines, and by which so many have been deceived and are still being deceived today: I believed I could do nothing more useful than to share the Talent <note>A reference to the biblical Parable of the Talents; the author views his knowledge as a divine gift that must be invested or shared rather than buried.</note> entrusted to me by the Father of Lights <note>original: "Patre luminum." A common theological reference to God as the source of all enlightenment and truth (James 1:17).</note> with the sons and heirs of knowledge, so that even posterity

<summary>In this preface, the author warns against the many fraudulent alchemical texts of his time and declares his intention to share his divinely-granted knowledge with true students of nature.</summary>

# PREFACE.

might observe that, not only in previous ages but even in this of our own, that singular philosophical blessing of **GOD** has not been denied to certain men. For certain reasons, it did not seem advisable to publish my name, as I do not seek praise for myself but strive to be useful to the lovers of Philosophy. Therefore, I leave the vain desire for glory to those who prefer **TO SEEM** rather than **TO BE**.

What I write here as an attestation of undisputed Philosophical truth—comprehended in a few words—has been drawn from the practical experience <term>practical experience: original "manuali experientia," emphasizing hands-on laboratory work over mere book learning</term> granted to me by the Most High <note>original: "Altissimo," referring to God</note>. I do this so that those who have laid the first and real foundations in this most praiseworthy science may not, through this encouragement, abandon the practice of these most beautiful things; and thus they may be safe from the wicked crowd of Smoke-sellers <term>Smoke-sellers: "Fumivenduli," a common derogatory term for fraudulent alchemists who promised much but produced only smoke and deception</term>, for whom it is sweet to deceive. These are not dreams, as the ignorant common folk say, nor the empty fictions of idle men, as those foolish and unwise who laugh at the art claim. It is the very Philosophical Truth, which I, as a lover of truth, in

<meta>This page continues the author's preface from page 4. The text flows from the author's self-identification as a "lover of truth" into his justification for publishing these secrets despite the corruption of the present age.</meta>

# PREFACE.

...truth, as support and confirmation of the <term>Alchemical truth</term> <note>original: "veritatis Chymicæ." In this context, "Chemical" or "Alchemical" truth refers to the proven reality of metallic transmutation and the Hermetic philosophy.</note> which has been so unjustly decried, I neither should nor could hide it or wrap it in silence. This is so even though in these malignant times (where virtue and vice are held as equals), this truth greatly fears to come forth into public view on account of the unworthiness of the age and the ingratitude and faithlessness of men—to say nothing of the curses of the Philosophers <note>Ancient alchemical masters often "cursed" their successors to ensure that the secrets of the Stone remained hidden from the greedy or the profane.</note>.

I could bring forward witnesses of this Alchemical truth—namely, the most serious authors—following the unanimous consensus of venerable Antiquity across so many different Nations. But, those things which are seen by eye-witness experience <note>original: "experimento oculari." Literally "ocular experiment," emphasizing the importance of first-hand laboratory observation over mere book-learning.</note> require no further proof. Many men, of both the highest and lowest rank, have seen her—that is, **DIANA**—without her clothes <note>original: "sine veste **DIANAM**." In the language of alchemy, Diana represents the Moon or Silver. To see her "without her clothes" is a poetic way of saying one has witnessed the pure, naked essence of the matter or the secret of the work stripped of its allegorical veils.</note> in these past years (and I speak as one who knows).

And though there may be found certain <term>mischievous idlers</term> <note>original: "maleferiati." This term refers to those who use their leisure poorly; in the world of alchemy, it specifically targets "sophisters" or charlatans who spent their time deceiving others.</note> who—either out of envy, or malice, or fear that their own impositions might be detected—cry out that the soul can be extracted from gold and, through a pompous and vain delusion of display, be returned to another body, not without great loss of time, labor, and expense...

# PREFACE.

loss and detriment: let the <term>Sons of Hermes</term> <note>original: "Hermetis filij." A traditional title for alchemists or seekers of wisdom who follow the teachings of the legendary Hermes Trismegistus.</note> know for certain that the <term>extraction of souls</term> <note>original: "animarum... extractionem." In alchemical theory, the "soul" refers to the combustible essence or "sulfur" of a metal, which carries its color and transmutative power.</note> as they call them—whether from *Gold* or from the *Moon* <note>original: "Luna," meaning Silver.</note> (by whatever common *alchemical* way)—is a mere delusion. This is indeed not believed by many, but at length it is verified through experience (the one and only mistress of truth), though only at great cost. On the other hand, he who can perform this by the *Philosophical way* without fraud or deceit—so that he realistically <term>tinctures</term> <note>original: "tingat." To tincture or "dye" a metal was the alchemical term for permanent transmutation, changing its inner nature and outward color.</note> even the smallest amount of metal with the color of the *Sun* <note>Gold</note> or the *Moon* (remaining permanent in all required tests, whether with profit or without)—I can rightly affirm that he has the doors of *Nature* open to investigate further and higher secrets, and to acquire them with the blessing of **GOD**. Furthermore, I offer these present Treatises, written from my own experience, to the sons of science, so that while they are engaged with every thought and movement of the mind in inquiring into the hidden operations of *Nature*, they may perceive from them the truth of things and *Nature* herself; and that they may know in which single *Thing* the whole of the most holy *Philosophical Art*

<summary>The author warns against fraudulent alchemical methods for extracting the "souls" of metals, asserting that true success is found only in the "Philosophical way" confirmed by laboratory experience. He offers his own writings as a guide for serious students to understand the singular hidden reality behind Nature's operations.</summary>

<keywords>Sons of Hermes, extraction of souls, gold and silver, alchemical fraud, philosophical way, transmutation, nature's secrets, laboratory experience</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page's footer, concluding the definition of where alchemical perfection lies and transitioning into a warning against false practitioners and "mountebanks" who deceive the public.</meta>

# PREFACE.

perfection consists, provided they proceed by the **Royal Road** <note>original: "via Regia." The "King's Highway" or most direct, natural path in alchemy.</note>, which *Nature* prescribes in all her operations.
Therefore, I would wish the sincere reader to be warned here, that he may understand my writings not so much from the husk of the words, but from the forces of *Nature*, lest he later deplore time, labor, and expense spent in vain. Let him consider that this is the science of the **Sages** <note>original: "Sophorum." From the Greek "sophoi," meaning wise men or masters of the art.</note>, not of the unskilled: And that the meaning of the Philosophers is far too different for it to be perceived either by boastful **braggarts** <note>original: "Thrasones." A reference to Thraso, a character of a swaggering soldier in Roman comedy; used here to describe arrogant, loud-mouthed amateurs.</note>, or learned mockers, or those corrupted against their own conscience (who, since they cannot rise by their virtues, attempt to do so through crimes and slanders against honest men), or unskilled **mountebanks** <note>original: "circumforanei." Literally "market-crawlers"; traveling quacks or street-peddlers who sold fraudulent medicines and alchemical secrets.</note>, who with their **whitenings** <note>original: "dealbationibus." Superficial chemical processes used to give base metals a silver-like appearance without changing their true nature.</note> and **reddenings** <note>original: "rubefactionibus." Superficial processes used to give metals a gold-like tint.</note>, to the most unworthy defamation of the highly praised chemical science, have deceived almost the whole world. For it is the gift of **GOD**, since it can be reached by nothing except the grace of God alone illuminating the intellect, through patient and religious humility, or through the visual demonstration of an experienced teacher.

# PREFACE.

...demonstration. For this reason, God deservedly drives far away from His secrets those who are estranged from Him. As for what remains: I specifically ask the **Sons of the Art** <note>original: "filios artis." A traditional term for dedicated practitioners of alchemy who view themselves as spiritual heirs to the ancient masters.</note> to receive with a grateful spirit my desire to be of service to them. And when, by God’s will and through constant labor, they have made the **HIDDEN MANIFEST** <note>original: "OCCVLTVM fecerint MANIFESTVM." A fundamental alchemical principle involving the revelation of the internal essence or "seed" of a substance through the laboratory process.</note> and have arrived at the desired **Philosophical port** <note>original: "portum Philosophicum." A nautical metaphor common in the 17th century representing the successful completion of the Great Work or the attainment of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, let them follow the Philosophical custom and exclude all unworthy people from this art. Being mindful of the love they owe their needy neighbors in the fear of God, and having cast aside all vain boasting, let them sing eternal praises of gratitude to **God, the Best and Thrice-Greatest** <note>original: "optimo ter Maximo." A phrase combining the Roman address for the supreme deity (Optimus Maximus) with the "Thrice-Greatest" epithet of Hermes Trismegistus, used here to honor the Christian God as the source of all chemical wisdom.</note> for this special gift. Let them do so without abuse, holding this joy within a silent and piously rejoicing heart.

->*Simplicity is the seal of truth.*<-
<note>original: "Simplicitas veritatis sigillum."</note>

<summary>This concluding page of the preface warns that divine secrets are withheld from the ungodly and charges successful alchemists to use their knowledge charitably and secretly. It emphasizes that the ultimate goal of alchemy is a gift from God, requiring both physical labor and spiritual piety.</summary>

<image-desc>An ornamental woodcut headpiece sits at the top of the page, featuring symmetrical scrolls, floral motifs, and leaves. Below the titles, the main body of text begins with a large, square decorative woodcut initial 'M', filled with intricate foliage patterns.</image-desc>

### FIRST TREATISE.

# ON NATURE
## WHAT IT MAY BE,
## *and what kind of seekers*
## *it ought to have.*

**M**any wise and most learned men, many centuries ago—indeed, as **Hermes** <note>original: "Hermete teste." Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many foundational alchemical texts were attributed.</note> testifies, even before the flood—wrote many things concerning the preparation of the **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: "lapide Philosophorum." The legendary substance said to be capable of transmuting base metals into gold and acting as a universal medicine.</note>, and have left us so many writings that unless Nature worked wonders before our eyes every day, hardly anyone would now believe Nature to be real. This is because in former ages there were not so many "inventors" of things <note>original: "inuentores rerum." The author implies that ancient seekers were fewer but perhaps more genuine than the modern practitioners who invent complex, artificial theories.</note>. Nor did our predecessors consider anything other than Nature herself, and the **possibility of Nature** <term>possibilitas Naturæ: the inherent potential within the natural world to undergo change or perfection without artificial interference.</term>. And although they remained only in the simple path of Nature, they nevertheless found those things which we now, in so many thi-

<meta>catchword: res (things)</meta>

<summary>This page marks the beginning of the First Treatise, where the author introduces Nature as the primary teacher of alchemy and contrasts the simplicity of ancient masters with the over-complication of modern practitioners.</summary>
<keywords>Nature, Hermes Trismegistus, Philosopher's Stone, ancient wisdom, natural possibility, alchemical seekers</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the "First Treatise" on Nature from page 9. The header switches to "DE MERCVRIO" (On Mercury), though the text continues the philosophical argument regarding the human tendency to overcomplicate the simple operations of Nature.</meta>

<page-num>10</page-num>
<header># ON MERCURY</header>
<note>Although the header reads "On Mercury," the text continues the discussion of "Nature" from the previous page, illustrating how Mercury was often viewed as the primary "nature" or "spirit" that alchemists sought to understand.</note>

...[engaged in] things, could hardly have imagined with our own wits. This happens because **Nature** and the **Generation of things** <note>original: "Generatio rerum." The natural process by which things are born, grow, and are transformed.</note> in the world now seem cheap and simple to us. And therefore, we direct our intellects not toward things already known, but toward those things which cannot, or can hardly, be done.

Wherefore it also turns out for us that we can more easily devise subtle things—and imagine things which the **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophi." Here referring to the ancient alchemical authorities.</note> themselves did not know—than arrive at the true course of Nature and the meaning of the Philosophers.

And thus the nature of humans is fashioned: it neglects those things it knows and always seeks others. This is even more true of human intellects, to which Nature is subject. For example, you see any **craftsman** <note>original: "artificem."</note>, when he has already arrived at the highest perfection of his craft, he either seeks other things, or misuses his skill, or plainly stops. 

So also noble Nature always acts, even into the **Iliadus** <term>Iliadum: In Paracelsian philosophy, this refers to the ultimate state or the "final limit" of a thing's natural development.</term> itself—that is, the **Final Limit**—and afterward it ceases. For there was instilled in Nature a certain permission from the beginning, so that through its complete course it might reach better things and find full rest, toward which it [tends] with every effort...

<meta>The text cuts off at "ten-" which, according to the catchword "dit" on the previous page, completes the word "tendit" (tends/aims) on the following page.</meta>

<summary>This page argues that humans tend to overlook the simplicity of Nature in favor of complex, impossible theories. It introduces the concept of the 'Iliadus' as the final goal or state of rest toward which all natural processes strive.</summary>
<keywords>Nature, human intellect, craftsman, Iliadus, final limit, generation of things, alchemical philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues the comparison between ancient and modern alchemy. It picks up mid-sentence from the previous page, completing a thought about how Nature proceeds toward its final limit.</meta>

<page-num>II</page-num>
<header>## ON MERCURY</header>

...[procee]ds, and rejoices in its own end, not otherwise than the **Ant** in its old age, for whom Nature creates wings in its senescence <note>A reference to the folk belief that ants grow wings when they are about to die; here used as a metaphor for a process reaching its final, most "spiritualized" stage.</note>. Thus, indeed, our own wits have progressed so far, especially in the **Philosophical Art** <term>Arte Philosophica: A traditional name for alchemy, emphasizing its status as a sacred science rather than just a mechanical craft.</term>, or the practice of the **Stone** <note>original: "lapidis praxi." Referring to the preparation of the Philosopher’s Stone.</note>, that we have now almost reached the **Iliadus** <term>Iliadum: In Paracelsian philosophy, this refers to the ultimate state or the "final limit" of a thing's natural development.</term> itself. For the **Art of Chemistry** <term>Ars Chymiæ: While "Alchemy" often refers to the theory, "Chemistry" here refers to the experimental and laboratory-based methods.</term> now finds such subtleties that greater ones can hardly be discovered; and it differs from the Art of the ancient Philosophers as much as a **Clockmaker** <note>original: "Faber Horologiorum."</note> differs from a simple **Blacksmith** <note>original: "Fabro Ferrario."</note>. And although both work with Iron, nonetheless neither would understand the other's labors, even though both are Masters in their own art. 

If the father of Philosophers, **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of the alchemical tradition.</note> himself, were to come back to life today, along with the subtle-minded **Geber** <note>An 8th-century figure (Jabir ibn Hayyan) known for his rigorous laboratory methods.</note> and the most profound **Raymond Lully** <note>Ramon Llull (c. 1232–1315), a major philosopher and mystic to whom many alchemical works were attributed.</note>, they would be regarded by our modern Chemists not as Philosophers, but rather as students. They would not know so many of the **distillations** <term>distillatio: The process of purifying a liquid by heating it into vapor and then cooling it back into a liquid.</term> used today, nor so many **circulations** <term>circulatio: A process where a substance is continuously distilled in a closed vessel, mimicking the "circulation" of elements in nature.</term>, nor so many **calcinations** <term>calcinatio: Heating a solid to high temperatures to drive off volatile substances or reduce it to a powder.</term>, nor so many other innumerable works of Artists which the men of this century have discovered and devised based on their writings. Only one thing is lacking to us: that we might know that...

->A 6<- <note>This is a signature mark, used by early printers to help bookbinders assemble the pages in the correct order.</note>

<summary>The author argues that modern chemistry has far surpassed the technical skills of ancient masters like Hermes and Geber, comparing the advancement to the difference between a blacksmith and a clockmaker.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy vs. Chemistry, progress of science, Hermes Trismegistus, Geber, Raymond Lully, Iliadus, distillation, laboratory techniques</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 11, completing the comparison between ancient masters like Geber and modern alchemists, then transitions into a formal definition of "Nature" as the singular instrument of God.</meta>

<page-num>12</page-num>
# ON MERCURY

...which they themselves made, namely the **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: "lapidem Philosophorum."</note> or the **Physical Tincture** <term>Tinctura Physica: A liquid form of the Philosopher's Stone, believed to have the power to "tinge" or transform base metals and heal the human body.</term>. While we seek that Stone, we find other things; and if human procreation were not so common, and if Nature did not maintain its own law in that matter, we would by now have nearly gone astray. 

But to return to the purpose: I promised in this first Treatise to explain **Nature**, lest a vain imagination deflect us from the simple, true way. 

I say, therefore, that **NATURE** is one, true, simple, and whole in its being, which God made before the ages and enclosed a **spirit** within it. Know, however, that the **Limit** of Nature is God, who is also the principle of Nature, since no beginning is finished except in that in which it begins <note>This reflects the Hermetic idea of the Ouroboros (the snake eating its tail), where the end of a process returns to its divine source.</note>. I have said it is only one, through which God does all things—not because God would not know how to work without it (since He Himself made Nature and is omnipotent), but because it pleased Him so, and so He did. 

All things proceed from that one Nature, nor is there anything in the world outside of Nature. And although sometimes **failures** <note>original: "abortus." In this context, it refers to "monstrosities" or unsuccessful natural developments.</note> occur, this is not the fault of Nature, but rather the fault of the **craftsman** <term>artifex: The alchemist or "artist" who attempts to mimic or accelerate natural processes in the lab.</term> or of the place. This Nature is divided into places...

<meta>catchword: four,</meta>

<summary>The author defines Nature as a singular, divinely-inspired force that serves as the medium for all creation. He argues that failures in alchemy or biology are the fault of the practitioner or environment, not Nature itself.</summary>
<keywords>Nature, Philosopher's Stone, Physical Tincture, God as creator, artifex, natural law, alchemy vs. nature</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 12, further defining Nature as a divine instrument that operates through the four qualities and requires the alchemist to follow the law of "like acts upon like."</meta>

<page-num>13</page-num>
# ON MERCURY

...four <note>Likely referring to the four elements or qualities described in the preceding text.</note>, in which she performs all these works that appear and exist in shadow—for things exist in shadow rather than appearing in their true form. She changes into male and female and is likened to **Mercury**, because she joins herself to various places; and according to whether the locations in the earth are good or bad, she produces things—though there are no truly "bad" places in the **Earth** <note>original: "Terra."</note>, as it might seem to us. 

There are, however, only four **qualities** <term>qualitates: In the natural philosophy of the time, these were the primary forces of Hot, Cold, Moist, and Dry. Their proportion determined the character of all physical matter.</term>, and these are present in all things; they do not exist in harmony, for one always exceeds another. **Nature** herself is not visible, even though she acts visibly, for she is a **volatile spirit** <note>original: "spiritus volatilis."</note> who performs her duty within physical bodies. She has her place and her seat in the **Divine Will** <note>original: "voluntate Diuina."</note>. 

For our purposes in this place, she is useful for nothing else than for us to know her "places"—those that are most suitable or closest to her—that is, so that we may know how to join one thing to another according to **Nature**. We must not mix wood with man, nor an ox or any animal with metal; instead, let each thing act upon its own likeness, and then Nature will also fulfill her duty. The "place" of Nature is nowhere else than what was stated above: in the will of God <note>original: "in velle Dei."</note>.

<summary>This page defines Nature as an invisible, volatile spirit acting according to the Divine Will through four primary qualities. It emphasizes the alchemical principle of "like acting upon like," arguing that successful work requires joining compatible substances.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, Nature, four qualities, volatile spirit, Divine Will, compatibility of substances, alchemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues from page 13, which described Nature as a volatile spirit acting within the Divine Will. This section moves from defining Nature to defining the character and methodology of the "Seeker" who wishes to work with her.</meta>

<page-num>14</page-num>

# ON MERCURY

Those who seek out Nature <note>original: "Scrutatores Naturae."</note> ought to be of the same character as Nature herself: truthful, simple, patient, and constant; and what is most important, they must be pious, God-fearing, and not harmful to their neighbor. Afterwards, let them carefully consider whether their proposal accords with Nature, and whether it is even possible. Let them learn from visible examples—namely, from what substances a thing is made, in what manner, and in what kind of vessel Nature operates. 

For if you simply wish to do something just as Nature herself does, then follow Nature; but if you strive to perform something more excellent than Nature has done, observe in what and by what means a thing is improved, and it will always be accomplished within its own kind. 

For example, if you desire to advance **metal** <note>original: "metallum."</note> (which is our specific intention) further in virtue than Nature has already done, it is necessary to take the metallic Nature, and this in the **male and female** <term>mare & fœmina: In alchemical theory, all things are composed of active/masculine and passive/feminine principles, often equated to Sulfur and Mercury, which must be united for a successful "marriage" or transformation.</term>; otherwise, you will accomplish nothing. For if you intend to create Metal from herbs, you will labor in vain, just as you would not produce wood from a dog or any other beast.

### SECOND TREATISE.
# ON THE OPERATION
## OF NATURE IN OUR
->*purpose and seed.*<-

I have already said that Nature is true, unique, appearing everywhere, and continuous, and she is recognized by the things she produces—namely, woods, herbs, and so on.

I also said that the seeker of Nature <note>original: "scrutatorem Naturae"</note> must be truthful, simple, patient, and constant, applying their mind to at least one thing, etc. Now we must begin with the act of nature: just as Nature exists within the will of God, and God created her, or imposed her upon all imagination, so Nature made a seed <term>sperma: In alchemical philosophy, this refers to the "generative principle" or "seed" of a substance, containing its potential for growth and transformation.</term> for herself—that is, her will within the Elements. She is indeed one, and yet she produces diverse things; but she performs nothing without the seed. Whatever the seed wills, Nature performs, for she is like the instrument of some Artisan <term>Artifex: The 'Craftsman' or 'Artisan' is a traditional title for the alchemist, who does not create new things but assists Nature in perfecting her work.</term>. Therefore, the seed of each and every thing is better and more useful to the Artisan than Nature herself: For from Nature without the seed, you will accomplish only as much as—

### THIRD TREATISE.
# ON THE TRUE FIRST
## MATTER OF
### *Metals.*

---

The first matter of **Metals** is twofold, yet one without the other does not create a metal: The first and principal matter is the **Moisture of the air** <note>original: "Humidum aëris." Early scientists believed that air contained a subtle, life-giving moisture that served as the basis for all physical growth.</note> mixed with heat; this the Philosophers named **Mercury**, which is governed by the rays of the sun and moon in the **philosophical sea** <note>original: "mari philosophico." A symbolic term for the primordial, fluid state of matter before it is "fixed" into a solid form.</note>. The second is the **dry heat of the earth**, which they called **sulfur**. 

But because all true Philosophers have hidden this [sulfur] most of all, we shall explain it a little more clearly—especially the **weight** <note>original: "pondus." In alchemical literature, "weight" usually refers to the specific proportions or ratios of the substances used in a recipe.</note>, for if this is ignored, everything is destroyed. It is because of this that many produce "abortions" <note>A common alchemical term for a failed experiment or a substance that has failed to mature into its "perfect" state (like gold).</note> from a good starting material. For there are those who take the whole body as the matter, or the **seed** <note>original: "semine, vel spermate." The author uses biological terms to describe the concentrated essence of a metal.</note>, or the sperm; others take only a fragment; and all of these stray from the right path. For example: if someone were to take a man’s foot and a woman’s hand and wished to create a human being from this mixture, it would not be possible.

<summary>This page defines the "First Matter" of metals as a dual substance: a moist "Mercury" and a dry "Sulfur." The author emphasizes that understanding the correct "weight" or proportion of these ingredients is the only way to avoid failed alchemical experiments.</summary>

Alchemists often described the life-force or "First Matter" as a divine or celestial spark hidden within dense physical matter.</note> in the body, which is guarded by the rest of its body from all excessive heat and cold, etc. 

If you have ears and senses, pay attention here and you will be safe—not only from those who are ignorant of the location of the sperm and strive to reduce the whole grain into seed, but also from all those who occupy themselves with the **vain dissolution of metals** <note>Early chemists often failed because they tried to dissolve entire bars of metal into liquid, thinking the whole mass was "seed," rather than extracting the specific "mercurial" essence.</note> and wish to dissolve metals entirely, so that afterwards, from their mutual mixing, they might create a new metal.

# ON MERCURY.

take this warning for yourself: do not seek that "point" <note>Referring to the "point of Nature" mentioned on the previous page—the infinitesimal spark of life within matter.</note> in common metals, for it is not there. For these metals—especially **common gold** <note>original: "aurum vulgi." Alchemists distinguished between "vulgar" gold (the coins and jewelry we use) and "philosophical" gold (the living essence of the metal).</note>—are dead. Ours, however, are living and possess a spirit; these are the ones that must be used. 

For you should know that the life of metals is **fire**, as long as they are still in their ores within the earth; their death is also fire—specifically, the fire of the furnace <term>fusionis: the melting or smelting of metal, which alchemists believed "killed" the metallic life-force by fixing its form.</term>. 

The **First Matter** of metals is actually a certain moisture mixed with warm air; it takes the form of a "fatty water" <note>original: "aqua pinguis." A term for a viscous, nutrient-rich liquid that alchemists believed was the primordial substance of all minerals.</note> that clings to every substance, whether pure or impure. It is found more abundantly in one place than in another, which happens because the earth in certain locations is more open and porous, possessing a stronger attractive force than elsewhere. 

Sometimes this substance emerges into the light on its own, "clothed in a garment" <note>A metaphor for the physical appearance or crust that hides the true essence.</note>, particularly in places where it has nothing else to cling to. It is recognized in this way: although every created thing is composed of the **three principles** <term>tria principia: the influential theory that all matter is made of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</term>, in the matter of metals, there is only one principle present without any union—except for its "garment" or its shadow, namely **Sulfur**, and so on.

### FOURTH TREATISE.

# HOW METALS ARE GENERATED
## *in the bowels of the earth.*

Metals are produced in this way: after the four Elements have cast their virtues into the center of the earth, the **Archaeus** <term>Archaeus: derived from the Greek word for "ancient" or "beginning," this refers to the "inner artisan" or vital force that alchemists believed governed the growth of minerals and living things.</term>—through the heat of perpetual motion—distills and sublimates these virtues toward the surface of the earth. For the earth is porous, and the "wind" <note>In alchemical theory, "wind" often represents the volatile, gaseous state of matter before it condenses.</note> trickling through these pores of the earth is resolved into water, from which all things are born. 

Therefore, let the **sons of learning** <note>original: "doctrinæ filij." A common term for students of alchemy or those initiated into the hermetic mysteries.</note> know that the seed of metals is no different from the seed of all things—namely, a **moist vapor**. For this reason, practitioners of the art seek in vain to reduce metals back into their "First Matter" <term>materia prima: the primordial, formless substance from which all things were thought to be created.</term>, because that matter is nothing but a vapor. 

The Philosophers did not mean such a "First Matter" in their writings, but rather only the "Second Matter," as **Bernard of Treviso** <note>original: "Bernardus Trevisanus." A renowned 15th-century alchemist who spent his life and fortune seeking the Philosopher's Stone.</note> argues most excellently—though perhaps not so clearly, because he speaks of the four Elements. Nevertheless, he [says]...

sublimates <note>original: "sublimat." ### ->ON MERCURY.<-

...has purified, where the earth is fine, pure, and moist; it fills its pores and joins with it, and thus becomes the **Sun** <note>Gold; alchemists used the names of planets to represent metals.</note>. But if that **fatness** <term>fatness: (Latin: *pinguedo*) refers to the oily, combustible principle of sulfur that provides the "body" of a metal.</term> comes to impure, cold places, it becomes **Saturn** <note>Lead</note>; if, however, such earth is pure and mixed with sulfur, it becomes **Venus** <note>Copper</note>, and so on.

For the more a location is purified, the more beautiful the metals it returns. We must understand that this vapor continually moves out from the center to the surface; in its passing, it purifies those places. This is why today minerals are found in places where a thousand years ago there were none; for in its progress, it always refines the crude and impure, gradually drawing a little of it along with itself.

And this is the repetition and **circulation of Nature** <note>original: *circulatio Naturae*. This describes the ecological and geological cycle where matter is constantly refined through heat and movement.</note>. It is sublimated for so long, producing new things, until the location is perfectly purified—and the more it is purified, the more noble are the things it produces.

In winter, however, when the cold air constricts the earth, that **oily vapor** <note>original: *unctuosus vapor*</note> is also congealed. Later, when spring returns, it is mixed with earth and water and becomes **Magnesia** <term>Magnesia: In alchemical literature, this is not the modern chemical, but a "mother-earth" substance or a "magnet" capable of drawing down celestial influences.</term>, drawing to itself the **Mercury of the air** <note>original: *Mercurium aëris*</note> which is similar to it. This Mercury gives life to all things through the rays <note>Catchword: [of the] Sun</note>...

<summary>This page explains the subterranean formation of metals, arguing that Gold, Lead, and Copper all originate from the same vapor but differ based on the purity and temperature of the soil. It describes a continuous "circulation of Nature" that refines the earth over centuries to produce minerals and the vital "Mercury of the air."</summary>

->FIFTH TREATISE.<-
# ON THE GENERATION
## OF STONES OF EVERY
->*kind.*<-

The material of stone is the same as that of other things, and they are born according to the purity of their locations in this manner. When the four **Elements** <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water, the building blocks of all matter in the Aristotelian tradition.</note> drip their vapor into the center of the earth, and the **Archaeus of Nature** <term>Archaeus: (from the Greek *archē*, meaning 'origin') an internal vital force or "invisible craftsman" believed to direct the biological and chemical processes within nature.</term> ejects and sublimates that same vapor, it passes through the various places and pores of the earth. As it moves, it draws with it all the impurity of the earth up to the surface; this is later congealed by the air. For that which the air creates pure, the air also congeals.

# ON MERCURY.

...raw, because air has entry into air, and they join together, for **Nature rejoices in Nature** <note>original: "Natura Naturâ lætatur"; a classic alchemical maxim meaning that like substances are naturally attracted to and perfect one another</note>, and thus rocks and stony mountains are formed, according to the large and small **pores** <note>microscopic openings or channels in the earth through which vapors circulate</note>. And the larger the pores of the earth, the better that place is purified; for while a greater heat and a larger quantity of water pass through such a **breathing-hole** <note>original: "respiraculum"; a vent or passage in the earth's crust</note>, the earth is more quickly cleansed. Thus, afterwards, metals are more easily produced in such locations, just as experience itself testifies: for gold is sought and found nowhere else but in the mountains, which cannot happen on the plains except rarely. 

For very often such [plain] places are damp, not with vapor but with **Elemental water** <note>original: "aqua Elementali"; raw, physical water as opposed to the refined "philosophical" vapor that creates metals</note>, which draws that vapor to itself, and they embrace each other so closely that they are not easily separated. Afterwards, the **Sun of heaven** <note>the literal sun, acting as an external heat source</note> **digesting** <note>original: "digerens"; the alchemical process of slow heating to mature or ripen a substance</note> it, makes that **fat clay** <note>original: "argillam pinguem"; a dense, oily earth</note> which potters use. But the vapor [of water] ascends to its proper places and is congealed in pure water into stones, grain by grain.

As for how colors are produced in gems, it must be known that this happens by reason of sulfur in the following manner: if the fatness of sulfur is congealed, then by that perpetual motion, the spirit of water, in passing through, **digests** <term>digestion: a slow, steady heating process used to mature or "ripen" a substance over time</term> and purifies it by the power of salt, until, once the color is ripened, it is colored red or white. This color—tending toward a more perfect state along with that spirit because it has been **refined** <note>original: "subtiliatus"; made thin, pure, or subtle</note>—is raised with the spirit through so many repeated **distillations** <note>the process of vaporizing and condensing, which alchemists believed purified substances through cycles of ascent and descent</note>. The spirit then has the power of entering into things...

# ON MERCURY.

...matter. Now, truly, we shall begin a discourse concerning seed and the things generated from seed. A central alchemical theory suggesting that the life-force or "spirit" is a type of celestial air that becomes trapped and solidified in physical bodies.</note> in some body, or a moist vapor, and unless this is also dissolved by a warm vapor, it is of no use.

Therefore, let the investigators of the Art know what seed is, lest they seek a thing which does not exist. Let them know that it is threefold, produced from the four elements. The first is **MINERAL**, which is the subject of our discourse here; the second is **VEGETABLE**; and the third is **ANIMAL**.

The **MINERAL** seed is known by the **Philosophers** <term>Philosophers: (Latin: *Philosophis*) the "Natural Philosophers" or alchemists who seek to understand the hidden, inner workings of the natural world.</term>. The **VEGETABLE** seed is common and ordinary, as we see in fruits. The **ANIMAL** seed is known through **imagination** <note>original: *imaginatione*. In this context, "imagination" refers to a formative, creative power of the mind or soul that influences the generation of living beings.</note>. The energetic, active essences that provide life and growth to plants and animals.</note> in trees...

# ON MERCURY.
**B 4**

This opens the pores of the tree and causes drops to fall, always separating the **pure** from the **impure**. 

However, sometimes the pure comes before the impure: the pure remains and congeals into **flowers**, while the impure turns into **leaves**, and the coarse and thick parts become **bark**. The bark of the tree remains fixed; the leaves fall due to cold or heat when the pores are blocked; but the flowers are congealed into whatever color the heat itself dictates, and then the tree bears **fruit** and **seed**.

Consider the **apple**, for example, in which there is **sperm** <note>original: *sperma*. In early modern biology and alchemy, 'sperm' often refers to the fleshy part of the fruit or the protective casing, rather than the reproductive 'seed' itself.</note>. A tree is not born from the flesh of the apple, but within that flesh is the **seed** or grain intrinsically, from which a tree can be born even without the surrounding fruit. For multiplication happens not in the outer sperm, but in the inner seed. Thus, we see with our own eyes how **Nature** creates seed from the **four elements**, so that we might not labor in vain at this task; for that which is already created does not require a creator. This I have given by way of example...

# ON MERCURY.

Because this seed is invisible, it is often not believed to exist in the **Order of Nature**. 

But it is no wonder that the uninformed doubt; for if they cannot perceive what is right before their eyes, they will perceive much less that which is invisibly hidden. However, it is a most certain truth that what is above is nothing other than that which is below, and vice versa <note>This is a reference to the famous alchemical axiom from the *Emerald Tablet*: "That which is below is like that which is above."</note>; that which is born above ground arises from the same source as that which is born below in the bowels of the earth. 

And what special privilege do **vegetables** have over **metals**, that God would implant a seed in them while undeservedly excluding metals? Are not metals of the same dignity before God as **trees**? Let this be established as a certainty: **nothing grows without a seed**, for where there is no seed, the thing is dead. 

It is necessary, therefore, that either the **four elements** create the seed of metals, or they produce them without a seed. If they were produced without a seed, then they could never become perfect, since—

# ON MERCURY.

—cury will be made clear by the following example. It is manifest that humans have a seed, in which they are multiplied: The human body is **Mercury**, but the seed is hidden within the body, and in proportion to the body, its quantity in weight is very small. Therefore, he who wishes to generate a human being should not take Mercury, which is the body, but rather the seed—which is a congealed vapor of water. 

In this way, common chemists proceed wrongly in the regeneration of metals: they dissolve metallic bodies—whether **Mercury**, **gold**, **Saturn** <term>Saturn: the alchemical name for Lead</term>, or **the Moon** <term>the Moon: the alchemical name for Silver</term>—and they corrode them with strong waters <note>original: *aquis fortibus*; corrosive mineral acids like nitric acid</note> and other foreign substances <note>original: *heterogeneis*; materials that do not share the same nature as the metals</note> not required for the true art. 

Afterward, they join them together, not knowing that a human being cannot be generated from a dissected human body, because the corruption of the body and the destruction of the seed has already occurred in this manner. Every thing is multiplied through the male and the female, as I mentioned in the treatise on the double matter. The separation of the sexes creates or produces nothing, but their proper conjunction brings forth a new form: therefore, seeds or sperm are to be taken, not the bodies.

There is one God; from this one God, the Son was begotten. One gave two, and two gave one—the **Holy Spirit** proceeding from both. Thus was the world made, and thus shall be its end. Consider the previous four points most exactly: you have in them the Father, the Father and the Son, and finally the **Holy Spirit** <note>original: "SS." abbreviating Spiritus Sanctus</note>. # CONCERNING MERCURY.

...there is the Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal kingdom. The Mineral can subsist by itself, even if there were no humans in the world, nor any tree or herb. But the third indeed has life from the two aforementioned, without which it could not exist; it is more noble and excellent than the two aforesaid, just as it is also the last among them and dominates them, because virtue is always finalized in the third, and multiplied in the second <note>The author suggests that the "Animal" kingdom is the completion of nature's power, while the "Vegetable" (or "Second") kingdom is where the power of growth and multiplication is most visible</note>.

Do you see this in the Vegetable Kingdom? The <term>first matter</term> <note>original: *prima materia*; the raw, unformed essence of a substance as it exists in nature</note> is the Herb or the Tree, which you do not know how to create; Nature alone does this. In this kingdom, the <term>second matter</term> <note>original: *secunda materia*; the refined or reproductive form of a substance, such as a seed</note> is the seed, which you can see; in this, the Herb or Tree is multiplied. 

In the Animal Kingdom, the <term>first matter</term> is the Beast or the Human, whom you do not know how to create; but you know the <term>second matter</term>, or the <term>sperm</term> <note>original: *sperma*; used here in the broader biological sense of a reproductive "seed" or generative principle</note>, in which it is multiplied. 

In the Mineral Kingdom, you do not know how to create a metal, and if you boast of it, you are vain and a liar; Nature has made this, and altho—

In the vegetable kingdom, the seed appears on the outside; the "kidneys" of its <term>digestion</term> <note>original: *digestionis*; the process of ripening or maturation through heat</note> is the warm air. In the animal kingdom, the seed appears both inside and outside; the kidneys of its digestion are the kidneys of the male. In the mineral kingdom, the seed is <term>Water</term> in the center of their heart and life; the "kidneys" of its digestion is fire. 

The receptacle of the vegetable seed is the earth. The receptacle of the animal seed is the womb of the female. The receptacle of the air—the mineral seed—is the air. And these are the receptacles of the seeds, which are the congealings of the bodies: for that which is <term>digestion</term> is also <term>solution</term> <note>the breaking down of a solid into liquid</note>, and that which is <term>putrefaction</term> <note>the rotting or breakdown of matter to release its essence</note> is also destruction.

It is recognized in this way: because it does not naturally mix itself with anything outside of its own Kingdom, it is not dissolved but rather congealed, because it does not lack solution—

It is necessary, therefore, for the pores of bodies to be opened so that the *sperm* <term>sperma: the visible, physical fluid that acts as a carrier for the generative essence</term> may be emitted. In the center of this sperm lies the *seed* <term>semen: the invisible, spiritual "spark" or formative principle of life</term>, which is air. When that seed enters its proper *matrix* <term>matrix: the specific environment—such as soil, a womb, or a specialized alchemical vessel—required for growth</term>, it is congealed, and it congeals whatever it finds there, whether it be pure, or impure mixed with the pure. 

As long as the seed remains in the body, the body lives; when it is entirely consumed, the body dies. Moreover, all bodies are weakened after the emission of the seed. Experience itself testifies that men who indulge too much in Venus <note>original: "veneri"; a traditional reference to sexual activity or physical lust</note> become weaker, just as trees that bear too much fruit later become sterile. 

The seed, therefore, as has been repeated many times, is an invisible thing, but the sperm is visible, and it is almost a living soul; it is not found in dead things. It is drawn out in two ways: gently and with violence. But because the discussion here concerns its power, I say that nothing is born without seed, and all things are made by the power of the seed. Let the *sons of the art* <term>artis filij: a traditional term for true alchemical practitioners who understand the secrets of nature</term> know that it is in vain to seek the seed in cut-down trees, for it is only found in those that are green and living.

### THE EIGHTH TREATISE.
# ON ART, AND
### HOW THROUGH ART
### *nature operates in the Seed.*

Every seed by itself is of no importance if it is not placed, either by Art <term>Ars: the practice of alchemy, where the human operator assists or accelerates natural processes</term> or by Nature, into its proper matrix. And although the seed itself may be more noble than any creature, nevertheless its matrix is its life, which causes the sperm <term>sperma: the physical carrier or grain</term> or the grain to putrefy <note>In alchemy, putrefaction is the necessary first stage of transformation, where a substance breaks down so its inner life-force can be released.</note> and causes the coagulation of the pure point <note>original: "puri puncti"; the concentrated essence or "spark" of life within the matter</note>. Furthermore, by the heat of its own body, the matrix nourishes the seed and makes it grow. This occurs in all three of the aforementioned kingdoms of nature <note>The Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal kingdoms.</note>, and it happens naturally over months, years, and seasons.

However, that Art is wise which can shorten the process in the mineral and vegetable kingdoms, though not in the animal kingdom. In the mineral kingdom, Art perfects only that which nature cannot, because of the "crude air" <note>original: "aërem crudum"; the raw, unbalanced atmospheric conditions on the Earth's surface compared to the protected environment deep underground</note> which, by its violence, has filled the pores of every body—not in the bowels of the earth, but on the surface of the earth, as I have said before in the preceding chapters. But so that it may be more easily underst[ood]—

<header>ABOUT MERCURY.</header>

...stood this, I wished to add further that the Elements, as if in competition with one another, cast their seed into the center of the earth, just as if into the kidneys <note>original: "renes"; in early modern physiology and alchemy, the kidneys were often associated with the production and filtering of generative fluids.</note>. The center, through its own motion, then sends that seed out into a matrix <term>matrix: a "womb" or receptive environment, such as a specific type of soil or rock, where a substance can grow</term>. These matrices are indeed innumerable; as many places as there are, so many matrices exist—one purer than another, and so on almost to infinity. Know therefore, that a pure matrix will produce a pure offspring in its own likeness. For example, among animals, there are matrices for humans, cows, horses, dogs, and so on; likewise, in the mineral and vegetable kingdoms, there are metals, stones, and salts. For salts must be considered within these two kingdoms, along with their locations, according to their greater or lesser degree of purity.

# ->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

I approach [this task]. The **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*</note> or **tincture** <note>A 'tincture' in alchemy refers to a potent substance—often the Stone itself—capable of transmuting base metals by "dyeing" or infusing them with its perfected quality.</note> is nothing other than **Gold** <note>original: *Aurum*</note> digested to the highest degree; for common gold is like a plant without seed, but when a plant matures, it produces seed. In the same way, when gold matures, it yields its seed or tincture. 

But someone may ask: why does gold or any other metal not produce seed? The reason provided is this: because it cannot reach maturity due to the **harshness of the air** <note>original: *aëris cruditatem*. Alchemists believed that the external environment and local temperature (the 'air') could either nourish or stifle the growth of metals within the earth's 'womb'.</note>; it lacks sufficient heat. It happens in certain places that pure gold is found, which nature intended to perfect, but was hindered by this harsh air. 

For example, we see that **orange trees** <note>original: *Pomorum Arantiorum*</note> grow in **Poland** indeed just like other trees; but in **Italy** and elsewhere, where the soil is their native home, they bear and yield fruit because they have sufficient heat. In these colder places, however, they do not at all; for where they ought to mature, they stop because they are overwhelmed by the cold, and thus their fruits are never obtained from **Nature** in these places—though sometimes Nature may be assisted gently and with ingenuity <note>original: *ingenio*. This refers to the 'Art' of alchemy, where human skill and technology (like controlled furnace heat) are used to complete what nature could not finish due to environmental limitations.</note>,

<meta>catchword: then (tunc)</meta>

<summary>This page defines the Philosopher's Stone as the "matured seed" of gold and uses the botanical analogy of orange trees failing to fruit in cold climates like Poland to explain why metals require specific "heat" to reach perfection.</summary>

--- Page 51 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page, which discussed the symbolic "Chalybs" (steel) and the maturation of gold, this page explains how the alchemist (the "Artificer") assists Nature through dissolution and the use of a secret solvent called "Our Water."</meta>

### ->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

Then Art now perfects what **Nature** <note>original: *natura*</note> could not have done. Exactly the same thing happens in the case of **metals** <note>original: *metallis*</note>. **Gold** <note>original: *Aurum*</note> is able to produce fruit and seed, in which it multiplies itself by the industry of a wise **craftsman** <note>original: *artificis*. The "artificer" or "artist" is the alchemist who uses their skill to speed up natural processes that would otherwise take centuries underground.</note> who knows how to promote Nature’s course. However, if he wishes to attempt this without Nature, he will go astray.

For not only in this Art, but in every thing, we can do nothing except assist Nature; and this is done by no other means than by **fire** <note>original: *igne*</note> or heat. But since this cannot be done—inasmuch as the spirits do not appear within a congealed metallic body—it is necessary that the body first be **dissolved** <note>original: *solvatur*. Dissolution is the "opening" of the metal, the first step in the Great Work, breaking down the solid form to reach the essence within.</note> and its pores opened, so that Nature may be able to operate. 

As to what kind of solution that ought to be, I wish the reader to be advised here that there is a double solution. Although there are many other solutions, they are useless: one only is truly natural, while the second is **violent** <note>original: *violenta*. Alchemists distinguished between "natural" dissolution (gentle, preserving the metal's "soul") and "violent" dissolution (using harsh, corrosive acids which they believed destroyed the metallic life).</note>, under which all others are categorized. 

The natural solution is of such a kind that the pores of the body are opened in **our water** <note>original: *aqua nostra*. This refers to the secret Philosophical Mercury, the universal solvent of the alchemists.</note>, whereby the digested seed may be released and placed within its own **matrix** <note>The "matrix" or "womb" refers to the specialized vessel or the chemical environment where the metallic seed is nurtured to grow.</note>. Truly, our water is a **celestial water** <note>original: *aqua caelestis*</note>, not wetting the hands; it is not the water of the common people, but is almost like rainwater.

This internal heat is much more effective than our common <term>elemental fire</term> <note>original: *igne elementali*. This refers to the ordinary fire found on the surface of the earth, which was considered a distinct, coarser element compared to the "celestial" or "vital" fire of nature.</note>, but it is tempered by earthly water, which day by day penetrates the pores of the earth and cools it. In the same way, the celestial Sun and its heat are tempered by the air, which day by day circles the world. If this were not so, by such heat...

# ->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

...domestic example: if you make water boil in a pot, you will see that with a slow fire come slow vapors and winds; but with a more vehement fire, thicker clouds will appear. Someone might object: if this were the case, then it should happen continuously, yet often no wind is felt. I respond that if water is not poured violently into a <term>distilling vessel</term> <note>original: *vas distillatorium*. In the traditional Aristotelian model of the universe, this was the region of pure fire located between the Earth's atmosphere and the moon.</note>, for fire does not endure water. 

You have an example before your eyes when you pour cold water into a hot furnace, from which "thunders" are triggered. This is because our eyes cannot penetrate [the clothes], and therefore our sight deceives us and accepts false things for true. But when their clothes are removed and they are stripped bare, so that they appear as nature formed them, each is easily recognized in their own sex. # TO THE SONS OF TRUTH

## PREFACE TO THE PHILOSOPHICAL ENIGMA.

I have now revealed everything to you, sons of science <term>sons of science</term> <note>original: *filij scientiae*. A standard address in alchemical literature for fellow initiates or dedicated students of the "Art."</note>, from the gushing spring of the universal fountain <note>The "universal fountain" often refers to the *Prima Materia*, the raw source of all things in nature.</note>, so that nothing more remains to be said. For in my previous Treatises, I have sufficiently explained nature through examples; I have manifested both the theory <note>original: *Theoricam*</note> and the practice <note>original: *praxin*</note> as far as was divinely permitted. 

But lest anyone should complain of Laconism <term>Laconism</term> <note>original: *Laconismo*. Named after the Spartans of Laconia, this refers to a style of speaking that is extremely brief or terse. The author is defending himself against the charge of being too concise or withholding information.</note>, as if I had omitted something, I will now describe the whole art to you once more in an enigmatic way <note>Alchemists used "enigmas" or riddles to hide their secrets from the unworthy while providing a clear path for the initiated.</note>, so that you may see how far I have reached under the guidance of God. There are infinite books treating this art, yet in hardly any will you find the truth revealed to you to such an extent. I wished to excel in this for the reason that, since...

# ->ON MERCURY.<-

I have conversed with many who believed they understood the writings of the Philosophers well, but I discovered that they explained those same writings with far more complexity than nature—which is simple—requires. Indeed, all my truthful words seemed too common and unbelievable to those who always pride themselves on "higher" wisdom. 

It has sometimes happened that I explained the Art to certain people word for word, but they were by no means able to grasp it, not believing that there is water in our sea <note>A common alchemical metaphor; "our sea" usually refers to the chaotic source of the work, and the "water" is the philosophical mercury that does not wet the hands.</note>, and yet they still wished to be seen as Philosophers. Since they could not understand my words even when spoken face-to-face, I do not fear (as other Philosophers have feared) that someone might grasp it too easily. It is a gift—of God, I say. 

It is true that if the study of Chemistry <term>Chymico</term> <note>original: *Chymico*. In this period, "chemistry" and "alchemy" were not yet distinct disciplines; the term encompassed both laboratory work and philosophical inquiry.</note> required a sharp and subtle intellect, and if the matter were something that could be seen with ordinary eyes, I have seen minds well-suited enough to investigate such things. But I tell you: be simple, and do not be overly "clever" until you have found the secret <term>arcanum</term> <note>original: *arcanum*. A secret or hidden mystery, specifically the core transformative substance or process in alchemy.</note>. Once you possess it, wisdom will necessarily follow. Then, you will not lack for infinite books...

When reading my writings, do not always cling to the literal syllables; rather, as you read, certainly consider nature and what is actually possible within her. Before you prepare yourself for the practical work, imagine diligently what it is you are seeking, and what the goal and end of your intention truly is. For it is much better to learn first with the mind and the imagination than with the hand and through great expense.

...or to hide away with **Diogenes** <note>original: *Diogene*. This refers to the Elixir or Philosopher's Stone, which was believed to be a universal cure-all.</note>, did not care to shorten their days, because before the eyes of every Philosopher the following life is as clear as your own face appears to you in a mirror. But if God shall give you the desired end <note>The "end" refers to the successful completion of the alchemical Work.</note>, you will afterwards believe me, and you will not reveal yourself to the world.

In alchemical lore, this mythical creature represents the power to "stop" or fix volatile substances, turning a liquid "spirit" into a solid "body."</note>, was also generated in those shores—the fish which so many men of high and low status have until now sought so diligently.

### ->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

He took the fruit of the Solar Tree <note>original: *Arboris Solaris*; in alchemy, the Solar Tree typically represents Gold or the "active" masculine principle.</note> and placed it in the vessel, and I saw the fruit of the Tree be consumed and dissolved like ice in warm water. I asked of him, "Lord, I see a wondrous thing: this Water is produced almost from nothing; I see the fruits of the Tree being consumed within it by such a gentle heat. To what purpose are these things?"

And he graciously replied to me: "My son, it is true that this thing is wondrous, but do not marvel, for it must be so. For this Water is the Water of Life <note>original: *Aqua vitæ*; here referring to a potent alchemical solvent, likely a purified form of Mercury, rather than brandy or spirits.</note>, having the power to so improve the fruit of this tree that afterwards—not by planting or grafting, but by its scent alone—it makes the other six trees <note>The "six trees" represent the other six metals of antiquity: Silver, Copper, Iron, Tin, Lead, and Mercury (if Gold is the Solar Tree).</note> uniform with itself.

Furthermore, this Water is like a female to this fruit; in no other thing can the fruit of this tree rot <note>original: *putrefieri*; in alchemy, "putrefaction" is a necessary stage of decay that allows for spiritual rebirth and purification.</note>, except in this Water. And although the fruit is wondrous in itself and a precious thing, nevertheless, if it rots in this Water, it gives birth from this putrefaction to a Salamander <note>A mythical lizard believed to live in and endure fire; here it symbolizes the "Philosopher's Stone" or the final stage of the work that is perfected by heat.</note> persisting in the fire. Its blood is more precious than any treasure, having the power to make the six trees you see here fertile, and to bring forth their fruit sweeter than honey."

But I asked, "Lord, how—"

How can this be? "I told you," he said, "that the fruits of the Solar Tree <note>original: *Arboris Solaris*; symbols of Gold or the perfected masculine principle.</note> are living and sweet; but while now barely one person is satisfied by it, once it is cooked in this water, a thousand can afterwards be satisfied by it." <note>This refers to the "multiplication" of the Philosopher's Stone, where its potency increases so it can transform vast quantities of base metal.</note>

I asked further, "Lord, is it cooked with a strong fire, and for how long?"

But he replied, "This Water has an internal fire <note>original: *intrinsecum ignem*; a "secret fire" or inherent energy within the chemical substance that allows it to transform from within.</note>, and if it is aided by continuous heat, it consumes three parts of its own body along with the body of the fruit. Nothing will remain except a most tiny part, which can hardly be imagined, yet it exists in the highest virtue <term>virtue: in alchemy, this refers to the concentrated power or medicinal efficacy of a substance</term>. It is cooked by the wise skill of the Master, first for seven months, and afterwards for ten. But meanwhile, various things appear, always around the fiftieth day, more or less."

I asked yet again, "Lord, can this fruit not also be cooked in other waters, or is something added to it?"

He responded, "There is no other Water but this one which is useful in this region or island. This paradoxical phrase is a classic alchemical description of "Philosophical Mercury," which flows like a liquid but does not behave like common water.</note>."

"Every creature uses it," he said, "but invisibly."

"Does anything grow in it?" I inquired.

And he replied: "From it all things in the world are made, and in it they live; yet in it there is properly nothing, but it is a substance that mixes itself with every thing."

I asked: "Is it useful without this fruit of the Tree?"

To this he said: "In this work, not at all, for it is only improved in this fruit of the **Solar Tree** <note>The Solar Tree represents the finalized alchemical gold or the "Stone" in its perfected state.</note> alone."

I began to plead: "Lord, I beg you, name it for me with such a clear name that I can no longer have any doubt."

But he, raising his voice, cried out so loudly that he woke me from my sleep; therefore I could no longer ask, nor did he wish to answer me further, and I can tell you no more.

And he replied: "It is true, son, all generation occurs through a male <note>original: "mare." This is likely a typographical error in the original Latin text or the transcription for "mas" (male), given the contrast with "female" (foemina) and the discussion of biological reproduction.</note> and a female; but because of the division of the three kingdoms of Nature, a four-footed animal is born in one way, and a worm in another way. For although worms have eyes, sight, hearing, and the rest of the senses, they are nonetheless produced through putrefaction <term>putrefactio: the alchemical stage of "blackness" where matter decays; alchemists believed this "death" was the necessary first step to spiritual rebirth</term>, and their place, or the earth in which they rot, is the female. So in the Philosophical Work <term>Opus Philosophicum: the "Great Work" of creating the Philosopher's Stone</term>, the mother of this thing is that Water of yours, so often repeated; and whatever is born from it is born in the manner of worms, through putrefaction. Therefore, the Philosophers created the Phoenix and the Salamander <note>In alchemy, the Phoenix represents the "Red Work" or resurrection, while the Salamander represents the "Fixed" substance that can endure the fire without being consumed.</note>. For if it were made from the conception of two [distinct] bodies, it would be a thing subject to death; but because it revivifies itself, once the former body is destroyed, another, incorruptible one shines forth. For indeed, the death of things is nothing other than the separation of one part from another. And in this Phoenix, it happens in such a way that life separates itself from the corruptible body by its own power."

Furthermore, I asked: "My Lord, are there different substances involved, or a complex composition of things in this work?" And he replied: "It is a single substance, with which nothing else is mixed, except for the Philosophical Water <term>Philosophical Water (Latin: *Aqua Philosophica*): The universal solvent or mercurial agent used to dissolve the 'body' of a metal to begin its transformation.</term> that has been revealed to you several times in your sleep. This water must be in a tenfold proportion to one part of the body <note>In alchemy, the 'body' usually refers to the solid metallic base, such as gold or lead, which is to be dissolved or 'perfected'.</note>. Experience, the only mistress of all things <note>A reference to the Latin proverb *experientia est magistra rerum*, emphasizing that practical work is the only way to prove alchemical theory.</note>, will be able to reveal this to you with the assistance of G O D." As I sought to ask further, he departed after bidding farewell without an answer; I was then awakened from sleep and restored to the longed-for region of Europe. And so, dear Reader, let what has been said by me so far suffice for you. Farewell.

# A DIALOGUE
## BETWEEN MERCURY,
## THE ALCHEMIST, AND
### NATURE.

At that time <note>original: *In illo tempore*. This phrasing mimics the beginning of liturgical readings or parables.</note>, the Alchemists gathered together and held a council to determine how they should create and prepare the **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>Philosopher’s Stone (Latin: *Lapis Philosophorum*): The legendary substance sought by alchemists for its ability to transmute base metals into gold and provide a universal medicine.</term>. They decided that everyone should take turns sharing their own opinion. This gathering took place under the open sky <note>original: *sub dio*</note> in a certain meadow on a clear day. 

Many agreed that Mercury <note>The liquid metal quicksilver, named after the messenger god for its fluidity and volatility.</note> was the first matter <term>First Matter (Latin: *materia prima*): The primordial, unformed substance that alchemists believed was the essential starting point for creating the Stone.</term> of the Stone; others argued for Sulphur, and others for different substances. But the prevailing opinion among them concerned Mercury, especially based on the writings of the ancient Philosophers, since they hold it to be the true first matter, and even the first matter of the Metals. For the Philosophers cry out: "**Our Mercury**" <note>original: *Noster Mercurius*. Alchemists often used this phrase to distinguish their secret "Philosophical Mercury" from the common metal found in a shop.</note> and so forth. And while they were thus contending among themselves through various labors—for each of them was waiting with desire...

<summary>This page introduces a group of alchemists meeting in a meadow to debate the identity of the "First Matter" needed to create the Philosopher's Stone. They conclude that "Our Mercury" is the most likely candidate based on the authority of ancient texts.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, Alchemist, Nature, Philosopher's Stone, First Matter, Sulphur, Alchemical Council</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the narrative from page 85, where an assembly of alchemists was discussing the nature of "Our Mercury" as the primary matter of the Philosopher's Stone. The previous page ended with the catchword "Mercu-".</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>

-ry and joy <note>original: "Mercurio & lætitia"</note>), meanwhile a great storm arose with squalls, clouds, and an unheard-of wind, which dispersed that gathering into various provinces, each one separately and without a conclusion. Nevertheless, each of them fixed in his mind what the end of that disputation ought to be. Therefore, each began his labors as before, one in this thing, another in that, seeking the **PHILOSOPHER'S STONE**, and this continues until now without ceasing. 

But one of them, mindful of the disputation—that the Philosopher's Stone <term>Lapis Philosophorum|The ultimate goal of alchemy, believed to transform base metals into gold and grant long life.</term> must necessarily be sought in Mercury—said to himself: "Even if no conclusion was reached, nevertheless I will work with Mercury, and I myself will bring it to a conclusion by making the blessed stone"; for he was a man who was always accustomed to speak to himself, as Alchemists usually do. <note>The "solitary alchemist" talking to himself is a common trope in early modern literature, reflecting the isolated and secretive nature of the "Great Work."</note> 

Therefore, he began to read the Books of the Philosophers, and he happened upon the Book of Alanus, <note>Likely referring to Alanus de Insulis (Alan of Lille), a 12th-century scholar to whom several alchemical treatises were attributed.</note> which treats of Mercury, and thus that Alchemist became a Philosopher, but without a conclusion. And having taken Mercury, he began to work; he placed it in a glass vessel <note>original: "vitrum"</note> and applied fire.

Mercury, as is its habit, evaporated. The wretched alchemist, ignorant of the nature [of things], beat his wife, saying: "No one could have come here but you; you have taken my Mercury from the glass." The wife, weeping, defended herself and said quietly to her husband: "You will make nothing but filth out of these things." <note>original: "merdam"; a vulgarity meaning dung or rubbish. The wife’s retort highlights the perceived futility and waste of her husband’s obsessive and messy experiments.</note>

The Alchemist took more Mercury once again and placed it back in the vessel, and so that his wife would not take it, he guarded it personally. But Mercury, in its usual way, evaporated again. The Alchemist, remembering that the **PRIMARY MATTER** <term>materia prima|The fundamental, formless substance from which the Philosopher's Stone is created.</term> of the **PHILOSOPHER'S STONE** must necessarily be volatile <note>In alchemy, a "volatile" substance is one that easily turns into vapor or "flies" away. The alchemist mistakenly takes the simple physical evaporation of quicksilver as a mystical sign that he is on the right path.</note>, rejoiced greatly. He convinced himself entirely that he could no longer be deceived now that he possessed this matter; he then began to handle the Mercury boldly.

Yet he could find nothing that served his purpose; he left nothing on earth untouched that he did not try to treat with "good Mercury."

No matter how many ways you turn yourself, you do not truly change; you only fashion a different form for yourself, and you always return again to your original state. <note>The Alchemist refers to mercury's ability to be vaporized or combined into various compounds and then recovered as liquid quicksilver—a property that both fascinated and frustrated early chemists.</note> **Mercury:** I do as you wish. If you want me to be a body, I am a body; if you want me to be a powder, I am a powder. I do not know how I ought to humble myself more than when I become powder and shadow. **Alchemist:** Tell me then: who are you in your center? If you tell me, I will torture you no more. **Mercury:** Now I am forced to speak with you from my very foundation. If you wish, you can understand me. You see my outer form; there is no need to speak further of that. But as for what you ask concerning my center: my center is the most <term>fixed</term> <note>original: "fixissimum"; meaning stable, non-volatile, and resistant to fire.</note> heart of all things, immortal and penetrating. In it is the rest of my Lord. I myself, however, am the way and the runner, both a stranger and a housemate; I am most faithful to all my companions...

<summary>Mercury describes his versatile nature, listing the various "essences" he can provide while defending his ability to return to his original form. He eventually reveals that his internal "center" is an immortal, stable heart, contrasting with his external volatility.</summary>

<keywords>Mercury, Alchemist, transmutation, fixed center, chemical properties, allegory, quicksilver</keywords>
